Tooling Q199 of 200
How to use GitOps for non-Kubernetes resources (Terraform, Ansible, CloudFormation)?
A) GitOps principles apply beyond Kubernetes: (1) Terraform: HCL in Git, Flux TF-controller or Atlantis reconciles to cloud. (2) Ansible: Playbooks in Git, Ansible Runner or AWX triggered by Git changes. (3) CloudFormation: Templates in Git, CFN Lint in CI + CloudFormation deploy via Git-triggered pipeline. The "GitOps Bridge" pattern: a reconciler that translates Git declarations into non-K8s API calls. GitOps is a METHODOLOGY — the principles (declarative, versioned, pulled, reconciled) work for any system
B) GitOps ONLY works with Kubernetes — it can't manage other infrastructure
C) Non-Kubernetes resources must use a separate deployment tool — GitOps is K8s-only
D) You need specialized "GitOps for Terraform" certification — standard GitOps doesn't apply
✅ Correct Answer: A
GitOps Beyond Kubernetes — The Bridge Pattern
  GITOPS BRIDGE (Adapts GitOps principles to non-K8s systems):
  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │                                                                │
  │  ┌──────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────────┐  │
  │  │   GIT    │──│  BRIDGE           │──│  TARGET SYSTEM       │  │
  │  │ (desired │  │  CONTROLLER       │  │                      │  │
  │  │  state)  │  │                   │  │  Terraform Cloud     │  │
  │  │          │  │ Pulls from Git    │  │  Ansible AWX         │  │
  │  │ HCL/     │  │ Translates to API │  │  CloudFormation      │  │
  │  │ YAML/    │  │ calls             │  │  DNS (Route53)       │  │
  │  │ JSON     │  │                   │  │  IAM (AWS)           │  │
  │  └──────────┘  └──────────────────┘  └──────────────────────┘  │
  │                                                                │
  │ Examples: Flux TF-controller (Terraform), Atlantis (Terraform),│
  │ Crossplane (K8s-native cloud resources via CRDs).              │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
  GitOps = METHODOLOGY, not a tool. 4 principles apply to ANY system.

GitOps is a methodology, not a K8s-specific tool. The 4 principles apply to ANY system. The "GitOps Bridge" pattern adapts GitOps to non-K8s systems.

🔬 GitOps Principles Applied — K8s vs Terraform vs Ansible
The 4 Principles Across Different Systems
  PRINCIPLE          │ KUBERNETES        │ TERRAFORM          │ ANSIBLE
  ───────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────
  Declarative        │ YAML manifests    │ HCL (.tf files)    │ YAML playbooks
  Versioned          │ Git SHA           │ Git SHA            │ Git SHA
  Pulled Automatically│ ArgoCD/Flux       │ TF-controller      │ AWX/Ansible Run
  Continuously Rec.  │ Every 3 min       │ Every 5-10 min     │ Scheduled/tagged
  ───────────────────┼───────────────────┼────────────────────┼─────────────────
  Reconciler         │ ArgoCD/Flux       │ Flux TF-ctrl       │ AWX Job Template
  State Store        │ Git repo          │ Git + S3 (tfstate) │ Git repo
  Target             │ K8s API Server    │ Cloud APIs         │ SSH/WinRM hosts
  ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  SAME 4 principles. DIFFERENT implementations. GitOps = universal methodology.
  For anihpj: Terraform for cloud infra + ArgoCD for K8s apps = Full GitOps.

For anihpj/jobpost: Apply GitOps to everything — Terraform (cloud infra via Flux TF-controller), Ansible (server hardening via AWX), DNS (Route53 via Terraform), IAM (AWS roles via Terraform). Git is the single source of truth for the ENTIRE system, not just Kubernetes.

Tooling Q200 of 200
How to build a GitOps Maturity Model for organizational adoption?
A) Maturity levels: Level 1 (Ad-hoc): Some YAML in Git, manual kubectl still common. Level 2 (Defined): All configs in Git, ArgoCD/Flux installed, basic reconciliation. Level 3 (Managed): PR review gates, auto-sync, drift detection + alerts, progressive delivery for critical services. Level 4 (Optimized): Multi-cluster hub-spoke, GitOps for infrastructure, image auto-updates, compliance as code, developer self-service. Level 5 (Pioneering): Internal Developer Platform, GitOps for everything, AI-assisted operations, industry-leading DORA metrics
B) GitOps is binary — you're either doing it or you're not; there are no maturity levels
C) Maturity models are for enterprises only — small teams don't need them
D) The highest maturity level is using only ArgoCD without any CI pipeline
